WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Unemployment and output both show a common pattern
of downturns being short, sharp and irregular, while the
subsequent recoveries and expansions are longer and
slower. The eminent economist Milton Friedman likened
this pattern to plucking on a string. The causes are unclear,

though there are some plausible candidates.
• If we take the “plucking” pattern seriously, there are clear
policy implications. Standard models used by central banks
do not allow for this pattern and therefore systematically
overstate how often capacity is tight. And when a
downturn does occur, the “plucking” view argues for
forceful stabilisation policy.
• We believe the current slow upward grind in
unemployment in Australia is a different phenomenon

and expect slow growth rather than a sharp downturn. If
people start thinking current inflation pressures demand a
1980s-style “Volcker disinflation”, though, it could just end
up plucking the economy.
Look at a graph of the unemployment rate for almost any
western economy, and you will notice a few things. The
big increases are sharp and sudden, while the subsequent
recoveries are slow and last longer. The timing of those upward
spikes is irregular, with no obvious pattern. The height of the

spike in unemployment is a good predictor of how far the rate
declines in the recovery that follows. By contrast, neither the
length of an expansion phase of declining unemployment, nor
the size of the decline predict the size of the next downturn.
This pattern is a far cry from the notion of a business
cycle, where output and unemployment oscillate round
some average or trend. There is something different about
downturns. They are not just like expansions but a bit slower.

The eminent economist Milton Friedman described this as a
“plucking” model. The economy, he suggested, was a bit like a
guitar string. Most of the time it stayed close to the fretboard.
There is a physical maximum amount of production possible
at any one time. Occasionally, though, the economy could be
plucked away from that maximum, with output falling and
unemployment rising sharply.
Remarkably, the mainstream macroeconomic literature that
central banks rely on has engaged little with this feature of the

data. Forecasting models used by the RBA and its peers do not
produce this pattern. Some authors have looked at “plucking”-
type models, though. Their analysis confirmed that downturns
predict the size of the following expansion, but the reverse
Is the economy ‘plucked’?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
is not true. Expansions do not “die of old age” by building up
imbalances. Australia went for 30 years without a proper
downturn: it turns out that this is entirely possible provided

nothing bad enough happens.
These authors further argue that the maximum production level
during expansions is buffeted by small shocks, some temporary,
others positive and permanent – think new technologies and
ways of doing things, or a policy change that encourages
workforce participation. Downturns, by contrast, are triggered
by large shocks that are usually temporary and arrive at irregular
intervals. Some argue that it is best to think of economies as

having three phases: normal expansions, which can continue
for long periods; shorter-lived downturns; and subsequent
recovery phases where growth can outstrip normal rates and
unemployment declines, but at a slower pace than it rose.
Some downturns do not end in full recovery, however. This
observation links to another strand of research showing that
the output losses associated with financial crises and wars are
not fully recovered. The experience after the Global Financial

Crisis shows this pattern in a number of advanced economies.
Standard models would interpret this as slower growth in
potential output, perhaps feeding the Australian tradition of
hand-wringing about insufficient productivity or reform.
While the pattern in the data is clear, the causes of “plucking”
are far from settled, with some authors seeing boom–bust
dynamics as inherent in the economic system rather than
driven by shocks. Some recent papers – including preliminary

RBA work – look to certain frictions in the labour market
for an explanation. These include search frictions, which
arise because workers and firms sometimes require time to
find a good match with each other. Another at least partial
explanation is that workers resist absolute wage cuts but
accept falls in purchasing power when dollar wages are
constant in the face of positive inflation.
An objection to this line of research is the finding by some BIS

researchers that the plucking pattern is clearer in countries
with more flexible labour markets – mostly English-speaking
ones. The literature focusing on labour market frictions also
does not explain where the originating pluck comes from,
“Downturns are usually short, sharp and
arrive at irregular intervals; expansions are
longer and slower."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
only why the data have the dynamics they do when those
shocks occur. Something must be causing the “pluck” shock,
if indeed it is a shock. The BIS paper suggests one possibility:
the authors find that output in the manufacturing and trade
sectors shows a clearer pluck pattern than construction.

Construction downturns tend to linger, perhaps because of
their connections to indebtedness and financial crises.
If we take seriously the patterns in the data showing
asymmetric downturns and the plucking pattern Friedman
described, there are clear policy implications. Currently, the
models underlying central bank forecasts are symmetric by
construction. They therefore systematically understate spare
capacity during expansions. They will find a positive output

gap ahead of a downturn, even when there was no evidence of
overheating at the time. This oversight risks policy mistakes.
The asymmetric nature of downturns also means that the
benefits of macroeconomic stabilisation policy are mostly
about cushioning downturns and hastening recoveries. In
normal times, the benefits will be small.
We also note that the RBA paper mentioned above hypothesised
a short-run NAIRU and a long-run NAIRU that are both pushed

around by actual unemployment in a path-dependent pattern.
This is an argument for “holding on to the employment gains”
that may have been missed by people whose mental model of
the economy is fully symmetric, without any plucking.
The plucking model started from empirical observations
about large downturns. The kind of slow grind upwards in
unemployment seen in Australia in the 2010s and in the past
couple of years does not fit this pattern. It is a qualitatively

different phenomenon requiring other explanations, including
perhaps the path-dependent drag upwards of the feasible
unemployment rate by the actual unemployment rate.
It is these kinds of considerations that underlie our current
forecast for slow growth and not a downturn, even though this
week’s labour market data were concerning. A further 25 basis
points or so of rate increases is unlikely to be the thing that
causes a severe downturn. Typically, downturns are not just

slower expansions but are qualitatively different phases that
require a trigger.
The one thing that makes me nervous is that the plucking
literature finds that every downturn is different. The famous
opening line to Tolstoy’s Anna Karenina, “Happy families are
all alike; every unhappy family is unhappy in its own way,”
would seem to apply to economies as well. And in at least one
case, a forceful tightening of monetary policy was the trigger
for a downturn. The so-called “Volcker disinflation” brought

US inflation under control in the early 1980s. That episode is
widely regarded as a success, despite the output costs. In the
current circumstances, some policymakers and commentators
might be tempted to believe that the same medicine is needed;
nobody wants inflation expectations to become entrenched,
after all. However, as RBA Board member Ian Ross has noted,
the institutions of the 1970s that produced wage–price spirals
are no longer operative. So there is no guarantee that a similar

approach nowadays would be necessary or as effective.
Watch out then, if the discourse turns to arguments that
countries “need a recession” to get inflation under control. A
Volcker-style policy could just end up plucking the economy.
Forecast changes
• We have upgraded our WPI forecast following this week's data
release, with our year-end forecast now 3.4%yr.
• We have also upgraded our near-term unemployment rate,
with a year-end forecast of 4.9%.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: risks mount
risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke
throughout the week also kept the focus on price risks, and called
for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again proposed a
50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at

the December and January meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and
continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not
of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales gave further
justification for this view this week, the control group
surprising to the upside with a 0.6% gain in October after
a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said,
housing is under considerable pressure, pending home sales

falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back at
2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent
weakness in this sector will, in time, have implications for both
consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international
development of the week. Again, it provided a call for pro-
active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales
growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr and the decline in fixed
asset investment broadened outside property, with capital

expenditure now down 8.1% across the economy year-to-
date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth;
though, even here, momentum softened in October, annual
growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not
only activity that remains under pressure, but also wealth,
with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective,

stimulus needs to affect change in both activity and wealth
and do so in a sustainable manner.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October,
employment falling 43.5k following September’s 36.4k
decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the
year have now slowed to 1.1%yr, materially below current
growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the
unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9%
despite the participation rate falling another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer
momentum in hiring, now we are seeing evidence of a material
increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have
remained in employment to date, but persistent job shedding
raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend in the
unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak
forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most certainly before the
late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price
Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3 increase was skewed
towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay
outcomes directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision
and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements meanwhile,
the most responsive to current and expected labour market
conditions, accounted for their smallest share of a Q3 wage
increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour

cost growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident
in housing. The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2%
to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005.
Interestingly, there remains considerable latent demand: almost
13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over the next
year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak
sentiment, high interest rates and expectations of further price

declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These
factors also pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for
inflation remained the critical theme. The October FOMC minutes
highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs
and energy prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several
[members] judg[ing] that price pressures were increasingly
extending beyond industries directly affected by data center

construction”. Participants "assessed that medium- and longer-
term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-
target inflation is a threat to the status quo, and perceived
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

consecutive shock in October, pushing annual

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Sunny side up
This week we published our final Economic Overview
for 2026. Titled “Sunny side up”, the report shows that
New Zealand’s economic recovery is continuing. Activity
expanded by 2.6% in the year to June, and recent indicators

point to further moderate growth. Overall, GDP is forecast
to grow by 2.8% in both 2027 and 2028. But this remains a
two-speed recovery: export-facing industries and regional
centres are enjoying stronger incomes, while households
and businesses tied to the major metropolitan centres are
still contending with high inflation, a soft labour market and
a subdued housing market.
The global backdrop remains broadly supportive. On an export-
weighted basis, New Zealand’s trading partners are forecast

to grow by 3.1% in 2026, 2.9% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028.
Stronger growth in the United States and East Asia outside
China, helped by the continuing artificial intelligence investment
buildout, is offsetting some of the drag from higher energy
prices. The central assumption underpinning these forecasts is
that a Middle East agreement eventually restores safe passage
through the Strait of Hormuz, allowing Brent crude to fall
towards US$77 a barrel in late 2027 and US$71 in late 2028.

Against that backdrop, the external sector is the clear bright
spot. Merchandise export volumes are estimated to have risen
by more than 4% this year, with dairy, meat and horticulture
joined by stronger output of manufactured metals, machinery
and equipment. Favourable growing conditions, high world
prices and a low New Zealand dollar have driven this growth.
Services exports are performing even more strongly and
are on track to grow by around 9% this year. Foreign visitor

arrivals continue to recover strongly, with Australians and
Chinese accounting for about two-thirds of the increase
seen this year. In 12-month rolling terms, visitor numbers
are expected to soon move above their 2019 peak, while
other services exports will likely continue their longer-run
outperformance.
Agriculture is expected to remain a major source of strength.
Farmgate returns are expected to stay elevated despite high
energy and shipping costs and an expected modest appreciation

in the New Zealand dollar. Meat export prices should remain
high in real terms over the next couple of years, with beef
supported by firm US demand. Kiwifruit and apple prices are
also expected to remain strong, while log prices should be
broadly stable to slightly firmer in 2027. Dairy prices – at least
powders – appear to have received an additional lift from El
Niño-related drought risk and strong global demand for protein.
We have raised our forecast milk price for the current season

by 50 cents to $10.25 per kilogram of milk solids – still ahead
of Fonterra’s own forecast, which was revised up to a $10.10
midpoint this week. We have also lifted our 2027/28 season
forecast to $9.75, reflecting a more gradual expected rise in the
New Zealand dollar than foreseen previously.
At this point, the strength offshore has not yet translated
into an equally convincing recovery for households. Average
household earnings grew only 2.9% in the year to June, while

high inflation pushed real wage growth into negative territory.
Real per capita household spending was flat over the year, and
weak house prices have limited growth in household wealth.
Spending should improve gradually during 2027 as export
income circulates through the economy, employment recovers
and inflation eases. Even so, the strongest gains are likely to
remain in regions directly exposed to agriculture and tourism.
The labour market has been unusually slow to respond to

improving GDP. Unemployment has climbed to an 11-year high
of 5.7%, and employment growth has failed to keep pace with
population growth. The pattern is best described as “low-
fire, low-hire”: firms avoided the scale of layoffs that might
normally accompany a downturn but may now have enough
staff to accommodate the early stages of a recovery without
immediately stepping up recruitment. Employment appears
to have begun turning higher and hiring intentions have

recovered, but unemployment is unlikely to decline materially
until next year. We expect it to ease to 5.1% by the end of
2027 and 4.5% in 2028.
Housing remains a restraint. National house prices have fallen
about 1.7% this year, sales have weakened and properties are
taking longer to sell. Adjusted for inflation, house prices are on
course for a fourth consecutive annual decline, and we expect
them to fall by around 33% between the 2021 peak and the

end of 2027. That adjustment is improving affordability and
supporting first-home buyers, but it also encourages existing
owners to save rather than spend. As with other economic
indicators, regional differences remain pronounced, with areas
benefiting from agricultural and tourism income outperforming
the major centres.
Residential construction should nevertheless rise during
2027, supported by a pipeline of more than 40,000 consented
homes, much of it medium-density development in Auckland

and Canterbury. The durability of that upswing is uncertain,
however, because financing costs, building costs and weak
house prices remain significant obstacles. Commercial
construction is closer to finding a floor, although office and
retail development is likely to stay subdued for a while given
elevated vacancy rates.
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Business investment has been more resilient. Capital equipment
spending rose strongly in the June quarter, and imports point to
further gains despite higher freight costs and a weaker currency.
Confidence rebounded after the initial shock from the Middle

East conflict and has held up despite renewed oil price pressure.
Investment will remain uneven, but cash-rich agricultural
businesses are well placed, and computing equipment and
software are contributing to growth.
Inflation is the main complication. Consumer prices rose 3.7%
in the year to September and annual inflation is expected to
reach 4.3% in December. Higher global oil prices have played
an important role, but the problem is broader than fuel. Core

inflation measures remain around 3%, operating costs are
elevated and firms’ pricing intentions have strengthened.
Headline inflation should fall as energy prices ease and could
temporarily dip below 2% in 2028, but underlying inflation is
expected to decline much more slowly.
That persistence means the RBNZ will likely need to tighten
policy by more than it currently forecasts. The OCR is expected
to reach 3% by year-end, but we think the neutral rate may

be closer to 3.75%, in contrast to the RBNZ’s 3% to 3.5%
estimate. As growth strengthens and core inflation proves
sticky, we expect the OCR to peak at 4% in September 2027
and remain there for about a year. Medium- and long-term
interest rates are therefore likely to settle above the unusually
low levels seen in the decade before the pandemic.
Finally, fiscal conditions should improve as stronger nominal
activity and above-target inflation lift tax revenue. However,

spending pressures and higher debt-servicing costs will be
difficult to avoid. We expect the operating balance before
gains and losses, excluding ACC (OBEGALx), to return to
surplus in 2028/29, while net core Crown debt peaks near
43.7% of GDP in 2027/28. The unresolved election adds
uncertainty, however; the forecasts assume no major near-
term change in policy direction.
Turning to this week’s key economic news, RBNZ Governor
Anna Breman and fellow MPC member Prasanna Gai spoke

to audiences in New Zealand and Australia respectively. We
thought that Breman’s comments were reasonably balanced.
On the hawkish side, Breman noted that some indicators of
economic activity had been stronger than expected. Perhaps
of greater note, she also said that this month’s partial October
CPI data (discussed below) indicated that indirect impacts
from the fuel price shock might be growing following a more
muted than expected impact in the September quarter. On

the dovish side, she noted subdued domestic demand, which
she said was making it difficult for firms to pass on higher
costs. She also referred to weak labour market data (including
weak wage growth) and the stability seen in medium-term
inflation expectations (also discussed below). Following the
October review Breman had commented that the chances of a
December rate hike were “high”.
In our view, Gai’s commentary leaned more on the dovish
side. He reiterated his concerns about the implications for NZ

of a bursting of the AI bubble and described recent inflation
and inflation expectations data as “quiescent”. In response to
a question, he indicated that he considered that news since
the October meeting had validated his decision to vote for a
pause at that meeting. The general tone of Gai’s commentary
suggested it would not be a surprise if he voted to extend the
pause at next month’s meeting. Even if he were to vote for
a hike, it seems likely that Gai would regard this as simply a

tentative step towards the roughly 3.25% neutral rate that the
Bank projected in the September Monetary Policy Statement.
On the data front, the partial CPI data for October pointed
to weaker than expected food price inflation but firmer than
expected increases in travel costs. The latter was probably
responsible for Breman’s comments above, but given the
month-to-month volatility of this data we would be hesitant
to read too much into one month’s outcome. These opposing

impacts netted out and so the outcome was consistent
with our forecast for a lift in annual inflation to 4.3% in the
December quarter. Meanwhile, the RBNZ’s long-running
survey of professional forecasters reported a lift in year-ahead
inflation expectations. However, encouragingly, the closely
watched 2-year-ahead measure was little changed at 2.36%.
Moreover, 5-year-ahead and 10-year-ahead expectations –
which had been singled out for special attention by RBNZ

Chief Economist Conway in a recent speech – fell to 2.17%
and 2.13% respectively. In both cases, these were the
lowest readings in almost two years. Finally, encouragingly,
the BusinessNZ services PMI continued its gradual trend
improvement in October, rising to a 3-year high of 51.7.

We expect this positive momentum to have carried into Q3 
2026, with further solid gains in residential building, private 
non-residential building and private engineering construction, 
particularly electricity-related projects. Public infrastructure 
investment will offset some of this strength, with the drag that 
began last year continuing as major projects reach completion.

The June quarter saw a larger than expected 0.5% fall in
retail spending, with high fuel prices squeezing households’
spending power. However, even with the drag from high

approvals through the first half of 2026 works through the
pipeline. Overall, we expect private capex to grow a strong
10.0%qtr in Q3, with data centres again leading the charge.

Est 3 for 2026/27 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with impressive nominal growth of 17.9%yr and
real growth of 15.3%yr. We expect Est 4 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $221bn, a 15.5% increase on a year ago,
indicative of the scale of investment yet to occur.

Document 372: National Bank of Hungary review_ The doves aren’t gone yet

Source PDF: all report pdf/National Bank of Hungary review_ The doves aren’t gone yet.pdf
Report source: ING Think

### Page 1

The National Bank of Hungary kept rates unchanged in
November, but delivered a dovish twist. A rate cut was already
on the table, and improving domestic risk sentiment is creating
more room for manoeuvre. Nevertheless, we only see the next
cuts coming in late 2027 as inﬂation pressures rebuild

Peter Virovacz and Zoltán Homolya
The National Bank of Hungary, Budapest
5.50% Key interest rate
(no change)
As expected
ING's policy view: A dovish twist, but no
rush

### Page 2

The National Bank of Hungary kept the base rate unchanged at
5.50% on 17 November, in line with widespread expectations. This
marked the third consecutive meeting with rates being kept on
hold. While the latest communication revealed somewhat more
room for monetary easing, we think the window of opportunity will
remain narrow.
Looking ahead, the exceptionally favourable domestic inﬂation
backdrop is likely to fade relatively quickly. We see inﬂation

accelerating to 3.0% by December 2026, with repricing at the start
of next year temporarily pushing price pressures closer to 3.5% in
the ﬁrst quarter of 2027. Inﬂation could then approach 4% during
the summer, largely due to base eﬀects. In our view, this
increasingly challenging inﬂation proﬁle means that any near-term
opportunity for easing may prove short-lived.
In the meantime, however, we see the latest dovish shift in
communication potentially testing the waters and gauging market

sensitivity to eventual easing, particularly in a global environment
where hawkishness still dominates. The key litmus test will be the
forint: a muted FX reaction to the dovish rhetoric would give the
central bank greater conﬁdence that some easing could be
delivered without jeopardising market stability. This means that a
possible one-and-done December rate cut has become a clear
dovish risk to our baseline.
However, we believe that any near-term window for easing would

be relatively narrow. As 2027 progresses, the main constraint on
monetary policy is likely to shift gradually from external risks
towards the domestic inﬂation outlook. With inﬂation expected to
move closer to 4% during the summer, we see little room for a
sustained easing cycle over the next six to nine months. Any near-
term easing would therefore be more likely to represent an
opportunistic move than the beginning of a conventional cutting
cycle.

A more durable easing window could open later in 2027, once
inﬂation starts to decelerate more convincingly after the summer. If
this coincides with a benign external environment and a further
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 3

decline in the risk premium on Hungarian assets, we see room for
one or two rate cuts in the fourth quarter of 2027. Our baseline
therefore sees the base rate reaching 5.00% by end-2027, followed
by a further reduction to 4.50% by end-2028.
Our three takeaways from the presser
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in

### Page 4

Review Policy rate NBH National Bank of Hungary
Monetary policy Hungary
1. A cut was already on the table. The biggest dovish surprise
came during the press conference. The Monetary Council
considered two options: keeping rates unchanged or cutting
them, while the decision itself was not unanimous. This
suggests that the bar for easing may already be lower than
previously thought. The December Inﬂation Report will now be
particularly important, with Governor Mihály Varga explicitly

stating that favourable developments in Hungary's risk
assessment and inﬂation outlook, together with reduced
external uncertainty, could increase its room for manoeuvre.
2. Hungary's risk story has improved. Perhaps the most notable
change in the narrative is the assessment of Hungary's risk
premium. Varga highlighted the favourable market reception of
the government's ﬁscal plans and its commitment to euro
adoption as factors supporting Hungary's risk assessment and

increasing room for monetary policy. The forint has also
remained stable and is stronger than a year ago, helping to
contain inﬂationary pressures. In other words, domestic
ﬁnancial market developments are becoming less of a
constraint on monetary policy than the Monetary Council
previously considered them to be.
3. The external environment still demands caution. The greater
room for manoeuvre does not mean that the NBH is ready to
embark on an easing cycle. Energy price volatility, elevated

global yields and above-target inﬂation in major economies
continue to warrant caution. At home, inﬂation is also expected
to accelerate from its current 2% level due to fuel prices, tax
changes and other policy measures. This leaves the Monetary
Council in a familiar data-dependent position: its room for
manoeuvre has increased, but whether it can actually use that
room will depend on both the external environment and the
December inﬂation outlook.

Document 386: Q3 Wage Price Index_ award wage boost (2026-11-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Wage Price Index_ award wage boost (2026-11-21).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#wages #inflation #labour
Q3 Wage Price Index: award wage boost
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
16:06 November 18 2026 Share
The minimum wage and award decision lifted wages growth, but unevenly – enterprise
arrangements responded more than individual arrangements, consistent with a softer
labour market. Q3 2026: 1.1%qtr, 3.5%yr.
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 1.1% in Q3 2026, slightly above Westpac’s forecast and the
market consensus for a 1.0% gain. That increased the annual growth rate to 3.5%yr – stronger than

the past six months, but a similar pace to 2025.
Today’s data also showcased the impact of the 2026 Fair Work Commission’s Annual Wage Review,
which handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award wages. This lifted wages in
aggregate, but the gains were uneven.
A significant share of the increase was driven by health care and social assistance, reflecting pay
rises for state government workers covered by enterprise agreements.
In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3. Awards and enterprise arrangements accounted for 64%

of the lift, while individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share for Q3 since 2019.
Enterprise arrangements, which are often directly linked to award outcomes, were more responsive,
while individual arrangements were less so compared to previous September quarters. That is
consistent with a softer labour market and recent business survey evidence that suggests the rise in
private sector labour costs following the award decision quickly normalised.

Overall, we see the Q3 result as a step up in the level of wages from the award decision rather than
evidence of building underlying wage pressure but we will be watching whether the strength in
enterprise bargaining persists.

### Page 2

Wages by Bargaining Stream
Westpac has long believed focusing on wage inflation by wage bargaining stream can help to
understand how wages respond to the economic cycle. Australia’s labour market is split into three
categories of pay setting methods:
Australian wage adjustments, including the Fair Work Commission’s (FWC) minimum wage and award
rate decisions, tend to occur at the start of the new financial year in July. The FWC handed down a
4.75% increase for 2026, larger than the decisions from 2025 (3.50%) and 2024 (3.75%), but lower

than in 2023 (5.75%).
In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3 2026. Of that increase, 0.50ppts were attributed to awards,
which was broadly in line with our expectations. However, both individual and enterprise arrangements
surprised us to the upside, contributing 0.64ppts and 0.65ppts to the quarterly increase in wages
respectively.
National minimum wage and award rates, regulated by the Fair Work Ombudsman,
Enterprise agreements, or
Individual arrangements.

### Page 3

Compared to the same quarter in previous years, each stream's contribution was the strongest in
absolute terms since 2023, largely because the overall lift was bigger than in 2024 and 2025. After
accounting for the size of the increase, enterprise arrangements were responsible for 64% of the lift,
whereas individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share since 2019.
While the larger award wage decision pushed up the overall increase, it did not flow through evenly.

Many enterprise agreements are directly linked to award outcomes, either through explicit reference to
award rates or because the award decision sets a benchmark in bargaining, which helps explain why
this stream was more responsive. By contrast, wages set under individual arrangements can be more
discretionary and depend more on employers' circumstances, so they were less responsive than in
previous years. That may reflect a softer labour market, where weaker demand for workers reduces the

pressure on employers to lift pay.
Wages by Sector
By sector, private wages (excl. bonuses) rose 1.0% in Q3, pulling up the annual pace from 3.1%yr to
3.4%yr. This marks the strongest pace of wages growth in the private sector since 2023, at the time of
the Fair Work Commission’s decision to raise award wages by a much larger 5.75%.
Private sector pay is set through a mix of arrangements, including a significant share of individual
arrangements (as discussed above). Today's result suggests this year's award wage decision may

### Page 4

have had some flow-on effect to the broader private sector, though, as noted, the pass-through was
uneven and appears to have been stronger for enterprise arrangements than for individual
arrangements.
There was also an element of both breadth and scale when it comes to private sector wage gains. Half
of all private sector jobs recorded a wage increase in Q3, the largest share since this series began in
2016; and of those jobs that recorded an increase, average hourly wages rose 4.5%, the strongest

since Q3 2023 (5.8%).
Public wages continue to outpace the private sector, this time with a 1.3% lift in Q3, bringing the annual
growth pace to 3.9%yr, the strongest since Q4 2025. As expected, the bulk of the gain was driven by
pay rises under enterprise agreements at the state government level, particularly for front-line workers.
Additionally, given scheduled rises under existing APS-wide enterprise agreements for Commonwealth
employees had already been delivered in previous quarters, the Commonwealth level contribution to

public sector wages growth during Q3 was modest.
Relative to the private sector, the driver of public sector wage gains looks to be more about scale than
breadth – across the 38% of public sector jobs recording a wage increase during the quarter the
average hourly wage change was 4.0%, slightly under the record high of a 4.3% recorded back in Q4
2023.

### Page 5

Summary
The Q3 result shows that the larger minimum wage and award rate decision boosted wages but the
detail suggests the lift was driven more by workers with stronger bargaining power. Awards and
enterprise arrangements, where pay outcomes are more directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s
decision or to collective bargaining, whereas individual arrangements, which are more exposed to
labour market conditions, contributed the smallest share since 2019.

That is consistent with a softer labour market – the unemployment rate has risen ½ppt since the start of
the year, and we are expecting it to peak at 5.2% mid next year. The NAB business survey points the
same way, with the increase in labour costs reported by private sector firms following the award
decision quickly normalising. This suggests the Q3 result is better read as a temporary step up in the
level of wages from the award wage decision than as evidence of building underlying wage pressure,

though we will be watching whether the strength in enterprise bargaining persists.

• The uptick in Thai GDP growth to 2.9% yoy in 3Q26 from 
2.4% yoy in 2Q masked a widening K-shaped divergence 
in growth. 
• The strong technology-led investment and export cycle 
will continue at a sustainable pace in 2027. 
• Private consumption will stay constrained by high debt, 
a weak labour market, and muted sentiment. 
• Fiscal space to sustain repeated stimulus measures and 
support household spending is narrowing.  
• Soft tourism is set for a seasonal recovery in 4Q26 and 
a gradual improvement in 2027. 
• We expect the BOT to remain on an extended pause 
throughout 2027, supporting an uneven economy. 
• Forecast implications: We expect real GDP growth and 
headline inflation to ease to 2.0% and 1.0%, 
respectively, in 2027, from revised forecasts of 2.4% 
and 2.1% in 2026 (2.1% and 1.7% previously).

2

Real GDP growth picked up to 2.9% 
yoy in 3Q26 from an upwardly revised 
2.4% in 2Q. However, the K-shaped 
divergence in growth widened. We 
forecast growth to ease to 2.0% in 
2027 from an adjusted 2.4% in 2026.

Net trade contributed positively in 3Q, 
as export expansion outpaced robust 
import growth, driven by 
supercharged demand for artificial 
intelligence (AI)-related electronics.

Overall growth gained momentum in 
3Q, but domestic demand was highly 
uneven. Investment surged, while 
private and government consumption 
were sluggish, although household 
spending edged up.

Higher GDP growth in 3Q was notably 
driven by stronger technology-
related industrial expansion, while 
the agriculture and services 
sustained their yoy growth.

Overall investment growth 
accelerated to 11.8% yoy in 3Q26, the 
strongest pace since 4Q12, with the 
private sector set to remain the 
primary driver.

Monthly data showed a sizeable jump 
in machinery and equipment 
investment in September. While this 
increase is unlikely to be sustained, 
we expect it to stay robust given the 
strong investment project pipeline.

Surging investment has been led 
notably by machinery and equipment 
outlays, underpinned by rising capital 
goods imports and local machinery 
sales relating to electronics.

Strong investment applications in the 
first three quarters of 2026 were 
driven mainly by the digital sector, 
which doubled from a year earlier, 
supported by favourable policies, and 
robust foreign investor interest.

across key ASEAN economies is 
strong heading into 4Q26 and early 
2027, supported by global AI 
tailwinds and the AI infrastructure 
build-out by major hyperscalers.

The US remains a major driver of 
Thailand’s strong goods export 
performance, with exports growing 
for the 27th consecutive month as of 
September 2026.

Thai telecommunications equipment 
exports are benefiting from the 
global AI boom, alongside other 
growing tech shipments. Electronics 
exports expansion should continue at 
a sustainable rate in 2027.

Thailand’s business sentiment 
remains positive, primarily supported 
by strong order books amid robust 
external demand, despite challenges 
including rising costs.

Private consumption remains 
sluggish, reflecting structural 
constraints from elevated household

any improvements has been 
temporarily boosted by stimulus.

Foreign tourism has faced a difficult 
2026, but should recover gradually 
into the seasonally strong year-end. 
Visitor arrivals will improve in 2027 as

has stalled, as households contend

6

Global food and oil prices remain 
above year-ago levels, amid supply-

weather conditions, and the 
unresolved conflict in the Middle East.

Headline inflation surged to 3.9% yoy 
in October 2026, the highest in 45 
months, driven by faster energy and

inflation will persist into early 2027 
before supply-side pressures fade.

Thailand’s upstream producer prices 
are facing substantial upward 
pressures, which is feeding through 
to consumer prices, but the extent is 
limited by muted consumer demand.

Thailand’s monetary policy is highly 
accommodative, with real interest 
rates falling despite a stable policy 
rate. We expect an extended policy 
rate pause at 1.00% in 2027 to 
support the uneven economy.

INFLATION AND MONETARY POLICY

### Page 7

Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence November 17, 2026 
7

Thailand’s current account was 
volatile and negative in the first 10 
months of 2026. Goods trade had a 
sizeable shortfall, and the services 
and income balances were in deficit.

External buffers will remain robust, 
despite falling import cover. This was 
driven mainly by surging imports, 
while foreign reserves have stayed 
resilient despite curbing FX volatility.

The decline in the goods trade 
balance reflected more than higher 
energy prices. It was also driven by 
higher capital goods imports from the 
technology-driven investment boom.

Portfolio flows have weakened and 
are volatile in 2H26 vs. strong inflows 
in 1H. Debt flows would be supported 
by stable domestic monetary policy, 
while equity flows stay volatile.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM) (5 
November)

As written in our Macro Strategy note, 
BNM’s final meeting of 2026 on 5 
November will be live and a close call, 
after the central bank opened the door to 
monetary policy normalisation during its 
September review. BNM Governor Abdul 
Rasheed Ghaffour reiterated in a 
Bloomberg interview on 15 October that 
the central bank has space to adjust 
policy if needed, as policymakers are 
vigilant about upside inflationary 
pressures amid strong economic growth. 
He also said that policy decisions will be 
data dependent. We think the latest 
available September data for the 
upcoming meeting suggest that 
policymakers can afford to be patient 
until 1H27, maintaining the Overnight 
Policy Rate at 2.75% for now. Inflation 
remained contained within the lower half 
of the official 2026 inflation forecast 
range of 1.5-2.5%, notwithstanding 
external price pressures and robust 
economic activity. Advance estimates 
showed GDP growth of 5.3% yoy in 3Q26, 
while exports jumped to a five-year high 
of 54.5% yoy in September, 
supercharged by the artificial 
intelligence boom. However, headline 
inflation eased slightly to 1.8% yoy in 
September 2026 from 1.9% yoy in August, 
while core inflation remained stable at 1.7% 
yoy. Fuel subsidies have limited the pass-
through of upstream cost pressures to 
consumer prices, alongside stable 
domestic demand conditions.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

South Korea

October trade and inflation data are due 
for release. Export growth is expected to 
accelerate to nearly 100% yoy, up from 
83.7% in September, while the trade 
surplus is forecast to remain above 
USD40bn for another month. CPI 
inflation is expected to hold steady at 2.9% 
yoy. Overall, the data should reinforce 
the view that AI- and semiconductor-
driven growth and the expansion in 
South Korea’s trade surplus remain 
firmly on track, providing fundamental 
support for the KOSPI and KRW. 
Meanwhile, inflation remains sticky 
above the BOK’s 2% target, bolstering 
the case for further monetary tightening 
as early as the November policy meeting.

Taiwan

October inflation data are forthcoming. 
Headline CPI inflation is expected to 
remain above 2% for the sixth 
consecutive month, at 2.3% yoy. Core 
CPI inflation is also likely to stay above 2% 
for the sixth consecutive month, at 2.3% 
yoy. Despite government subsidies on 
fuel and electricity prices, higher global 
energy prices continue to feed through 
to CPI via items such as airfares and tour 
package fees. Services inflation also 
remains sticky, particularly in food away 
from home, housing, transportation, and 
entertainment. Meanwhile, domestic 
inflation expectations appear to be 
gradually rising. A recent private-sector 
survey showed that the public expects

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

inflation to average 2.5% in 2026, with 
52% of respondents expecting inflation 
to exceed 2.5%. Against this backdrop, 
we maintain our view that the central 
bank will resume rate hikes in December, 
raising the policy rate by 12.5 bps to 
2.125%.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to rise to 3.2% yoy in October 2026, 
extending the increase over the previous 
two months, and moving slightly above 
the upper end of the Bank of Thailand’s 
1-3% target range. This was likely driven 
by faster energy inflation amid an 
uptrend in diesel prices, alongside a 
rebound in food inflation. The inflation 
uptrend was likely broadly in line with 
the central bank's expectations and 
mainly supply-side driven, supporting its 
decision to look through the temporary 
price pressures and keep its policy rate 
unchanged at its October meeting.

Vietnam

We expect Vietnam’s October 2026 
goods exports and retail sales to remain 
robust at 42.0% yoy and 14.0% yoy year-
to-date, respectively, while headline 
inflation is likely to stay elevated at 
around 5.0% yoy. Exports have 
continued to be supported by strong 
electronics and machinery shipments 
amid a favourable technology cycle with 
spillovers from robust artificial 
intelligence-related demand. Retail 
sales were likely bolstered by firm 
household spending, supported by 
favourable labour market conditions, 
and continued strength in tourism. 
Headline inflation has remained elevated 
and above the central bank’s target of 
4.5% for the year, driven by price 
pressures from transport and energy, 
food, as well as material costs. This 
poses a challenge for policymakers as 
they seek to balance inflation risks 
against ambitions of achieving at least 10% 
economic growth this year.

Hong Kong

Hong Kong’s economy is expected to 
decelerate from 4.3% yoy in Q2 to 2.6% 
in Q3, largely due to slower private 
consumption. Despite a 13% increase in 
Mainland visitor arrivals during the 
period, domestic consumption continued 
to be weighed down by a weakening 
labour market, with the unemployment 
rate elevated at 3.9% in September. 
Meanwhile, higher interest rates have 
dampened investment sentiment and 
capital market activity. The Centa-City 
Leading Index remained broadly flat, 
while the Hang Seng Index was range-
bound at around 25,000. The positive 
wealth effect seen in 1H has faded as 
asset market performance remained 
muted. A strong HKD continued to 
encourage outbound tourism, evidenced 
by an increase in average daily resident 
departures from 6.9% yoy in Q2 to 10.9% 
in Q3.

However, GDP growth is expected to be 
buffered by improving net exports amid 
the ongoing AI-electronics upcycle. 
Export growth picked up and outpaced 
import growth during Q3. Hong Kong, as 
China’s free port and re-export hub, is 
benefiting from stronger demand for 
Chinese electronic goods.

Indonesia/ Philippines

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Indonesia’s 3Q GDP likely moderated to 
5.2% y/y. Soft consumer confidence and 
retail sales growth point to weaker 
consumption, compounded by a gradual 
rise in inflation. Fiscal measures likely 
helped spending among low-income 
households. Despite the quarterly 
moderation, we maintain our full-year 
growth forecast at 5.3% y/y.

Inflation rates in Indonesia and the 
Philippines are expected to rise, albeit to 
different degrees. Indonesia’s inflation is 
on course to edge up modestly, while in 
the Philippines, stronger pass-through 
from higher oil prices, food and 
transportation costs, as well as imported 
inflation pressures stemming from a 
weaker peso, is likely to push inflation to 
a multi-year high.

健護 正向 民主黨贏得兩院對降低處方藥價及PBM監管持續，但法案協商的可能性
有利投保人數止跌回升。就財報及展望表現，仍優先看好製藥。
1
《製藥多受惠新藥銷售為成長動力》
適逢財報季及美國民主黨贏得兩院。就藥業影響，
降低處方藥價為兩黨共識，現已有IRA藥價談判及
最惠國藥價政策進行中，預期廠商有多元化產品線
可應對。在財報方面，標普五百健護財報公布迄今，

營收及獲利優於預期比重超過九成，在藥業，主要
受惠抗肥胖、腫瘤、免疫及神經科學新藥銷售增加，
在禮來成長動能來自國際市場及進入美國Medicare
市場，美國降價取得銷量成長，口服Foundayo今
年成長不如預期，明年及之後因全球陸續上市，仍
將穩定增加，後續雖有其他競爭者可能加入減重市
場，但因減重藥朝長期維持而非短期使用、適應症
擴 及 長 期 器 官 保 護 使 整 體 市 場 規 模 仍 高 。 嬌生
(JNJ)3Q26展現多線骨髓瘤藥物銷售呈現雙位數成

《醫療器材成長動能分化，預期明年漸出低谷》
美國兩黨對醫療器材的著墨度較低，預期不受國會

結構改變影響。就財報部分，3Q26醫材動能分化，
嬌生(JNJ)醫療科技部門營收成長僅2.5%，主要受惠
心血管類成長4.1%，但被受中國帶量採購(VBP)、
中東衝突干擾標案時程的一般外科拖累；亞培(ABT)
受左心耳封堵器觀望等待新品推出及血糖監測器國
際降價影響，下調全年營收增速至約6%，兩家公司
同樣期待明年不利因素緩解，加上多款新產品陸續
推出，預期將緩步走出低谷。微創機器人手術於
3Q26全球手術量年增率為15%，預期4Q26全球手
術量年增率約14.5%，3Q26美國達文西手術成長放
緩至僅10%，公司表示主因基期影響，預期未來因
擴大使用計畫降價3~5%取得量的成長，門診手術
使用量增及新的適應症，將使美國市場呈現穩定或
改善。

於規模、重定價改善醫療成本，
雖4Q26醫療成本季節性續升，
但預期2027年改善，維持兩位
數獲利成長。
9000億美元。現ACA進行增強型補貼的恢復提案還
在進行中，須總統立法才能恢復，可以是談判交換
條件的一部分，醫療補助的刪減是已立法，分階段
生效，無法被推翻，只能延後刪減，其中對工作的
要求將於年底生效早於新國會就任，12/11臨時撥
款法案談判是可以納入談判作為局部修正的時間點。
若醫療補助刪減延後，將有利於醫院，若ACA補貼
部分恢復將有利於ACA型管理式醫療業者的投保人

數止跌回升。兩黨均支持對藥物管理福利機構(PBM)
的監管，已有法案讓Part D藥價與PBM的報酬脫鉤，
回扣必須全數轉交給計畫方，2028年生效，現業者
多將Part D回扣與價差收入轉為固定費用應對，對
激進者業務分拆的主張則可能以聽證、調查形式出
現而非馬上立法。但在州層級，阿肯色州禁止PBM
擁有藥局的法律在州政府上訴中，CVS已決定1H28
分拆藥局成兩家獨立公司應對。就財報部分，UNH
3Q26財報仍維持業者利潤優先於規模與核保紀律，
合約重定價與刪除不賺錢合約，運用AI改善營運效
率、提升服務品質，在藥物福利管理主動讓利降低
政策風險。聯合醫療保險(UHC)因保費重定價與醫
療成本管控抵銷投保人數萎縮，Optum Health退
出虧損市場，聚焦高回報價值導向醫療運營紀律，
使此兩業務部門利潤率提升優於預期。商業保險醫
療成本趨勢依然高，但已趨於穩定，醫療優勢計畫
(MA)利潤率已接近2%-4%目標區間上限，公司自
評2027/2028年接近75%會員可望維持在4星以上
方案。醫療補助仍虧損，將透過州費率調整將緩步

走出低谷。4Q26醫療成本趨勢雖因季節性因素走升，
為今年獲利低點，但明年多項利潤率改善可期，維
持兩位數獲利成長。

• 國防需求已由短期補庫存轉
為多年期擴產
《國防需求已由短期補庫存轉為多年期擴產》
美國主要航空與國防類股公布3Q26財報，中東衝突
再升溫導致國防需求高於商業航空。以下是主要廠
商的分析 :
雷神∶受益於飛彈回補庫存，3Q26取得超過810億
美元商用與國防新訂單，其中國防訂單590億美元，
包括約230億美元「戰斧」飛彈訂單與140億美元
「先進中程空對空飛彈」（AMRAAM）訂單，帶
動總積壓訂單較上季增加520億美元，累計訂單
3,400億美元，年增36%。在飛彈防空系統與商業
航空維修服務市場強勁帶動下，公司上調2026年營
收到973億美元、EPS 7.70美元(年增22%)。
洛克希德馬丁∶受益於飛彈與火控系統產量提升，

帶動3Q26營收與獲利優於預期。在PAC-3多年期合
約則推升下，積壓訂單增加616億美元達2,920億美
元。由於美國、中東、北約國家飛彈補庫需求將持
續數年，所以公司上調2026年營收到810億美元(年
增8%)、EPS 30.55美元(年增42%)。
奇異航空∶受益於LEAP交付、備件、維修及shop
visit增加帶動，商用發動機及服務營收年增21%，
3Q26營收優於預期。在商用航空備件、維修服務及
引擎交付需求優於預期，加上主要供應商材料投入
改善， 公司上調2026 年營收到508億美元、EPS
7.75美元(年增26%)。
總結，雖然國防類股近期財報受益於產能提升維幅
上調全年財測，反觀油價高漲對於美國航空運輸業
造成全面性的衝擊，所以工業維持中立評等。

Document 517: 中間選挙後の対米投資の注目点

Source PDF: all report pdf/中間選挙後の対米投資の注目点.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 11 月 11 日

調査レポート

中間選挙後の対米投資の注目点

～「5,500 億ドル合意」は引き続き案件選別とモニタリングに留意を

調査部 研究主幹 中塚伸幸 
〇トランプ政権下で米国の直接投資受入れは相応に進展しており、日本からの投資も同様である。国際収支で
は「グリーンフィールド投資」の把握は難しいが、他のデータ等を勘案するとこちらも堅調である模様だ。 
〇政府間で合意された「日米戦略的投資イニシアティブ」、すなわち 5,500 億ドル対米投資は、公表案件の事業
規模で約 2 割の進捗だが、資金拠出である契約締結済み協調融資額では 2%程度である。 
〇中間選挙後もトランプ政権の貿易・投資政策には今のところ大きな変化はなさそうだが、予断を許さない。
「5,500 億ドル投資」については、今後、米側から対応加速を求められても、採算性の審査基準を維持し、事
後モニタリングも着実に行うことが肝要。円安影響はマネージ可能とみられるが、市場動向に留意は必要。 
1. はじめに 
米国の中間選挙では、トランプ大統領の共和党が大敗した。2 期目のトランプ政権は関税による保護主義的な
貿易政策を推進するとともに、他国による対米直接投資を歓迎してきた。わが国も関税交渉の一環として「日米戦
略的投資イニシアティブ」による 5,500 億ドルの対米投資に合意し、これを実行中である。  
上下両院で民主党が多数となっても、今のところトランプ政権の貿易・投資政策が大きく修正される可能性は
小さいと思われるが、予断を許さない。今後の動向を注視する上での前提として、対米投資の現状を整理する。 
2. 米国への直接投資流入の現状 ～国際収支で全貌の確認は難しいが、AI 関連は堅調な模様 
まず、米国への直接投資流入額を国際収支統計から、株式資本（ネット）、再投資収益、負債性資本（ネット）
の合計額でみると、図表 1 のように、2025 年は前年からほぼ横ばい、2026 年上半期は前年比増加となっている。 
図表 1 米国への直接投資の流入額

（出所）BEA ”U.S. International Transactions and Investment Position”

### Page 2

ただし、トランプ大統領が歓迎する工場設備の新設・増設など、いわゆる「グリーンフィールド投資」の動向は、
このデータでは十分に把握できない。国際収支の「再投資収益」は米国内の外資法人の内部留保増加額（利益
蓄積額）であり、また「株式資本（ネット）」も多くの部分は外国資本による既存米国企業の買収額等であるため、
直接的に設備増強に結びつくものは限られる。 
グリーンフィールド投資の動向をみる上では、Financial Times が提供する fDi Markets のデータがあるが、
これを引用している FRB の 2026 年 6 月のレポート 1によれば、2025 年の国外から米国へのグリーンフィールド
投資（公表ベース）の件数は 2024 年比ではやや減少している。 
一方、同じく fDi Markets のデータを引用している UNCTAD の 2026 年 10 月のレポート 2によれば、2026
年上半期の米国へのグリーンフィールド投資（公表ベース）の金額は 2,000 億ドルを超え、前年同期比では 5%
減ながら、半期としては過去最高だった 2025 年上半期に次ぐ高水準となっている。このうち、40%以上は半導体
やデータセンターなど AI 関連の投資で、個別プロジェクトで最も金額が大きいのは TSMC の工場建設（2025 年
は 1,000 億ドル、2026 年は 765 億ドル）とのことである。すなわち、必ずしもトランプ政権の政策を反映して投資
流入が足元で大きく増えているわけではないが、AI 関連を中心に相応の対米投資は行われている、といえそう
である。 
3. 日本からの対米直接投資 ～ネットフローでは大きな変化ないが、グロスでは増加続く 
日本の国際収支統計から対米直接投資の動向を確認すると、図表 2 のように、ネットのフロー額（投資実行額
から投資回収額を差し引いた金額）では今のところ 2025 年以降に大きな変化はみられない。もっとも、グロスで
みた投資実行額は着実に増加が続いており、2025 年は 42.7 兆円と、2014 年以降で過去最高であり、2026 年
1～8 月累計も前年比 11%増となっている。ただ、同時にグロスの回収額も増加しているため、ネットでは 2025 年
は前年比減、2026 年 1～8 月累計でも前年比微減となっている。 
図表 2 日本の対米直接投資フロー

（出所）国際収支統計 （注）26 年は 1-8 月の累計

1 Kallen, C.(2026) ”Recent Developments in Foreign Direct Investment in the United States,” FEDS Notes, June 29. 
2 UNCTAD(2026), Global Investments Trends C. Monitor, No 51, October.

### Page 3

なお、上述の米国の国際収支と同様、ネットフローには在米日系企業の再投資収益（内部留保蓄積）や企業
買収などが含まれ、グリーンフィールド投資はこのうちの一部にとどまる。 
2025 年以降の個別案件では、ソフトバンクによるオープン AI への出資（2025 年 300 億ドル、2026 年 300 億
ドル）、日本製鉄の US スチール買収（2025 年 142 億ドル）、日本生命によるレゾリューションライフの買収（2025
年 84 億ドル）などの大型案件が実行されているほか、投資計画では、トヨタの工場設備増強（今後 5 年間で最大
100 億ドル）なども発表されている。 
また、2025 年以降の主なグリーンフィールド投資案件では、fDi Markets のデータを引用した JETRO の
2026 年 7 月のレポート 3によると、データセンター投資としてソフトバンクグループ（2 件で計 14 億ドル）および
NTT データ（2 件で計 10 億ドル）の案件があるほか、ソフトバンクグループによるオハイオ州でのガス火力発電
（330 億ドル、後述の「5,500 億ドル対米投資合意」の対象案件）、信越化学の石油化学品案件（34 億ドル）、JST
の電子部品案件（5 億ドル）、日立エナジーの変圧器案件（5 億ドル）、日東電工の化学品案件（4 億ドル）などが
挙げられており、AI 関連、電力関連を中心に、民間企業による対米投資は総じて活発といえるであろう。 
4. 日米合意「5,500 億ドル対米投資」の進捗状況 ～事業規模ベースでは約 2 割の進捗 
2025 年 9 月に覚書を締結した「日米戦略的投資イニシアティブ」、いわゆる 5,500 億ドルの対米投資は、民間
企業による投資を政府が約束したものではない。図表 3 の左上部分に示されるように、本件は国際協力銀行（以
下 JBIC）による出融資と日本貿易保険（以下 NEXI）の保証付きの民間金融機関融資を原資として、日本側の
投資法人を通じ、米国での事業主体である SPV（特別目的事業体）に資金を供給するスキームである。 
図表 3 「日米戦略的投資イニシアティブ」のスキーム

（出所）財務省国際局「日米戦略的投資イニシアティブの現状報告」 P5 
（財政制度等審議会 財政投融資分科会 2026 年 9 月 18 日 資料）

3 伊藤博敏「米国データセンター最新事情(2) 電力インフラ周辺に広がる事業機会」JETRO、2026 年 7 月 6 日、表 2

### Page 4

すでに、図表 4 に示す 7 件のプロジェクトが公表されているが、7 件の事業規模の合計額は最大の場合で
1,300 億ドルとなっており、これは 5,500 億ドルの 23.6%に相当する。ただ、日米の合意は「資本コミットメント
（Capital Commitment ）」であるので、合意額の進捗という点では、協調融資額（JBIC の出融資と NEXI 付保
の民間金融機関融資の合計額）でみるほうが適切かと思われる。その場合、協調融資の契約締結額は現状 110
億ドルで、5,500 億ドルの 2%程度である。 
協調融資による資金供給はプロジェクトの段階に応じて行われ、「初回投資では総見込額の 3%～1 割程度 4」
とのことなので、協調融資額は今後、増額されていくことになろう。また、現在検討中の投資案件が順次公表され
ていくものと見込まれる。ただ、現時点の出融資契約額は合意額の 2%であり、まだ先は長いといえよう。 
図表 4 「日米戦略的投資イニシアティブ」の公表案件

（出所）経済産業省、財務省、JBIC、NEXI 等の公表資料から作成  
（注）協調融資額は契約締結済みの融資限度額、JBIC の出資額も含む

5. 「5,500 億ドル対米投資」の今後の注目点 
今後の対米投資のうち、「日米戦略的投資イニシアティブ」（5,500 億ドル投資）について今後を展望すると、以
下の点に引き続き留意が必要であろう。 
(1) 採算性の確保 ～当然ながら案件の厳選と事業開始後のモニタリングが重要 
本スキームのカギは、言うまでもなく投資プロジェクトの採算である。案件採択のプロセスは、日米双方が参加
する協議委員会での協議を経て、米･商務長官を議長とする投資委員会が審査して大統領に推薦し、大統領が
決定する、という流れであり、協議委員会の場で日本側の意向も反映される、とされている。

4 財政制度等審議会･財政投融資分科会 2026 年 9 月 18 日 資料 財務省国際局「日米戦略的投資イニシアティブの現状報告」

### Page 5

合意履行期限である 2029 年 1 月まで残り 2 年 2 カ月であるが、現時点の公表ベースでは、進捗は限られる。
万が一、トランプ大統領が投資合意の履行を急かすことになったとしても、採算性の評価基準を緩めることがない
ようにしなければならない。また、公表済み 7 件のうち 3 件がデータセンター向けを念頭においた発電案件であ
るが、中間選挙後にデータセンターをめぐる環境が変化し得る点にも留意が必要であろう。 
 さらに、投資実行後の事業モニタリングも案件選別と同様に重要である。この点については、専門家レベルで頻
繁に協議を行うなど着実にフォローアップを実施する旨が財政制度等審議会･財政投融資分科会や大臣の国会
答弁で示されている。実効性のあるモニタリングが求められる。 
(2) JBIC、NEXI のリスク許容力 ～運用次第だが、必ずしも過大なリスクテイクではない 
投資資金回収の最終的なリスクを負うのは、出融資をする JBIC と民間金融機関融資を保証する NEXI であ
る。いずれも政府機関であり、かりに回収に支障があれば財政負担になりかねない（なお、JBIC の「償還確実
性・収支相償性の原則」および NEXI の「収支相償性の原則」がそれぞれ遵守される点は、財政制度等審議会･
財政投融資分科会や大臣の国会答弁等で表明されている 5）。 
 5,500 億ドルを両者がどの比率で負担するかは案件ごとの判断だが、これまでの公表分では、約 3 分の 1 が
JBIC、約 3 分の 2 が NEXI 保証付き民間融資となっている。かりに、5,500 億ドルを 1 対 2 の比率で按分し、1
ドル＝158 円で換算すると、JBIC は 29 兆円、NEXI は 58 兆円となる。 
これは、図表 5 に示すように、JBIC 全体の出融資承諾額が 2025 年度で 2.5 兆円（米国向けは 1.3 兆円）、
同じく NEXI 全体の保険引受実績が同 8.4 兆円（米国向けは 0.6 兆円）であるのと比較すると、決して小さい金
額ではないが、両社の純資産、業務内容に鑑みると、必ずしも過大なリスクテイクというわけではないだろう。繰り
返すが、案件選別とモニタリング次第といえる。 
図表 5 JBIC と NEXI の事業実績

（出所）JBIC データブック、NEXI 年次報告書

5 財政制度等審議会･財政投融資分科会 2025 年 12 月 5 日 説明資料 財務省理財局

### Page 6

(3) 為替リスク ～円安リスクはマネジブルか 
投資資金はドル建てであり、場合によっては本件が為替に円安方向の影響を与える可能性がある。この点、政
府によれば、資金供給はプロジェクトの進捗に応じて段階的に行うことから、「一度に多額の、円安を招くような規
模の資金調達は想定していない」（大臣答弁）6と説明されている。 
ドル資金の調達は円投のみに依存するものではなく、JBIC については、政府保証付きドル債の発行や（JBIC
は 2025 年度末で 292 億ドル（4.6 兆円）の政府保証付きドル債発行残高があり、2026 年 7 月にも 15 億ドルの
同債券を発行している）、融資額の原則 5 割を上限として外為特会からのドル資金借入もあり得る、としている。ま
た、民間金融機関融資については、一部案件では外銀 2 行の参加も表明されており、外銀の調達は円投によら
ない可能性も高い。したがって、円安リスクについてはある程度マネージ可能であるように思われるが、市場動向
をにらみつつ対応することが必要であろう。 
以 上

線上法人說明會重點摘要

豐興 
(2015 TT, NT$62.8, 未評等)

焦點內容 
1. 3Q26 獲利季減 37%、年減 6%至 5.3 億元，或每股盈餘 0.92 元，主
因 1H26 提前補庫與淡季需求轉弱導致利潤率收縮。 
2. 預計 4Q26 旺季整體出貨季增，10-11 月接單已較 3Q26 有所改善。展
望 1Q27，在手訂單可望支撐出貨年增。管理層對鋼筋及型鋼相對有信
心，條線仍有變數。 
3. 若 11-12 月廢鋼價格續漲，型鋼有機會漲價反映成本，但條線漲價效
益可能延後至 12 月或 1 月顯現。鋼筋則受住宅需求偏弱及小鋼胚價格
增幅較小影響，調價空間有限。 
結論 
4Q26-1Q27 產品別需求及利差展望，僅型鋼較穩健 (營收佔比 20-25%)，
鋼筋偏弱，條線改善延續性有待觀察。在國內鋼筋需求未好轉、中國鋼材
出口保持高檔及廢鋼價格走升之情況下，公司利潤率改善空間可能限縮。

營運展望更新 
 預計 4Q26 旺季整體出貨季增，2026 年全年出貨約與去年持平。 
 鋼筋：預計 4Q26 出貨季增、年減。10 月出貨已較 9 月明顯回升，
但住宅需求仍偏弱。公司將安排在電費較具競爭力的時段生產，以
降低成本。 
 型鋼：預計 4Q26 出貨季增、年增雙位數，帶動全年出貨正成長。
9 月起客戶提貨意願改善，調價預期也有助下單動能。 
 條線：預計 4Q26 出貨年增雙位數。10-11 月接單已較 3Q26 略有
回溫 (3Q26 接單因客戶提前補庫而轉弱)。 
 展望 1Q27，在手訂單可望支撐出貨年增。管理層對於鋼筋及型鋼
相對有信心，條線仍有變數。鋼筋後續接單將視調價後的客戶採購
意願而定。 
 豐興 1-8 月鋼筋銷量年減 16%，跌幅大於全國鋼筋銷售量之 8.7%
衰退，主因公司 1H26 將部分資源策略性轉向條線及型鋼 (受原料
供應影響)，3Q26 又逢淡季與歲修。 
 條線下游以工具機關鍵零組件訂單較佳，螺桿、滑軌等工業自動化
零組件需求自年初以來維持較高水準。螺絲螺帽及手工具在 3Q26
表現較弱，10 月接單微幅改善。 
 若 11-12 月原料價格續漲，型鋼有機會透過漲價反映成本，條線的
漲價效益可能至 12 月或 2027 年 1 月才會較明顯。鋼筋利差相對
穩定，但需求偏弱、小鋼胚價格增幅較小，調價空間有限。

 受到國際廢鋼價格維持高檔及台幣貶值推升進口成本影響，公司在
10 月 22 日臨時調漲廢鋼價格 300 元/噸。 
 10 月一廠電爐突發故障，另一廠進行預防性停機，兩廠生產均受
影響，推升當月成本。 
 新竹土地重劃已完成，仍有少數地主須補件。公司將與 CBR 討論
招標時程，原則上最晚 1Q27 底前啟動，確切日期視進度而定。 
產業概況 
 世界鋼鐵協會預估 2027 年全球鋼需求將年增 1.7%至 17.5 億噸 
(對比今年持平於 17.2 億噸)，動能來自中國以外地區年增 4.0%，
抵銷中國需求因房市低迷而年減 1.0%。 
 2026 年 1-9 月全球粗鋼產量年減 0.7%；其中，中國年減 2.9%，
美國、印度及越南則分別年增 5.8%、5.6%及 29.1%。 
 國際廢鋼價格由年初約 345 美元/噸上漲至約 395 美元/噸，鐵礦砂
價格則較年初回落。日本廢鋼報價偏高，國內電爐廠仍以使用國產
廢鋼為主，進口則主要採購美國廢鋼。 
 10 月國內廢鋼基價為 9.9 元/公斤。豐興鋼筋、型鋼及條線與廢鋼
價差分別為 8.6、15.6 及 15.8 元/公斤。鋼筋價差相對穩定，型鋼
較 9 月回升，條線則由 7 月的 16.7 元/公斤逐步收窄。 
 台灣 1-8 月鋼筋表面消費量年減 10.7%。1-9 月建照樓地板面積年
減 12%；其中，住宅年減 29%、廠房年增 40%。同期新開工面積
年減 23%；其中，住宅年減 31%、廠房年增 18%。 
 台灣 1-9 月廢鋼、小鋼胚及型鋼進口量分別年減 19.7%、32.4%及
19.5%。盤元進口量方面，14 mm 及以下規格年減 1.1%，超過
14mm 規格年增 3.1%。管理層指出，房市偏弱及鋼筋消費量下滑
影響廢鋼與小鋼胚進口需求。 
 台灣 1-9月鋼筋出口月均量由去年同期的 4,706 噸升至 8,975 噸，
年增 90.7%。內需疲弱促使鋼廠積極爭取外銷，主要出口至澳洲。 
 中國 1-9 月成品鋼材出口年減 2.6%至 8,564 萬噸，月均量 952 萬
噸。出口自 2Q26 回升，3Q26 維持高檔至 10 月。4Q26 出口是否
續增，仍是國際鋼價的觀察重點。 
 中國 1-9 月半成品鋼材出口年增 46.5%，主因成品出口面臨反傾銷
措施，且伊朗鋼胚供應存在缺口，推升半成品需求。同期中國成品
鋼材進口年減 10.1%，半成品進口年增 26.6%。 
3Q26財報 
 3Q26 營收季減 9%，主要受到傳統淡季、歲修及天候影響，但因
鋼價高於去年同期，營收年增 4%至 68.8 億元。 
 受歲修及夏季電費影響，3Q26 毛利率季減 1.5、年減 1.2 個百分點
至 11.2%。稅後淨利季減 37%、年減 6%至 5.3 億元，折合每股
盈餘 0.92 元。獲利季減幅度較大，反映本業獲利下滑，以及業外
淨收益由前一季季 2.6 億元降至 0.8 億元。

Document 655: 抢跑 OpenAI 敲出史上最大 IPO，Anthropic 却惹怒了半个硅谷

Source PDF: all report pdf/抢跑 OpenAI 敲出史上最大 IPO，Anthropic 却惹怒了半个硅谷.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

五年前嫌  OpenAI 太商业化⽽出⾛的兄妹，敲出了史上最⼤  IPO 。
摘要：
11 ⽉  19 ⽇，成⽴仅仅  5 年的  AI 巨头  Anthropic 正式上市。
⼀举打破  SpaceX 此前创下的纪录，成为⼈类商业史上规模最⼤的⼀次  IPO 。
更夸张的是，开盘后它的市值更是⼀度冲破  2 万亿美元⼤关，收盘市值约 1.77 万亿美元。
魔幻的是，就在敲钟的同⼀天，近百位全球顶尖的  AI 研究者与创业者联名发布了⼀封⽕药味⼗
⾜的公开信《 The MisAnthropic Statement 》。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

这封公开信措辞相当激烈，很快就在社交⽹络上引发激烈交锋，甚⾄演变成⼀场公开骂战。
剧烈的舆论撕裂与⾸⽇抛压交织，让  Anthropic 的股价坐上了过⼭⻋。
盘中⼀度暴涨超过  37% ，随后⼀路回吐涨幅，最终收盘涨幅收窄⾄约  17% 。
但如果翻开  Anthropic 的招股书，你会发现⽐股价更有意思的，是它这次上市的制度设计。
从股权结构到员⼯套现安排， Anthropic 根本没按华尔街的传统规矩出牌。
表⾯看是⾦融操作，背后其实瞄准了硅⾕  AI 公司最头疼的两个问题：控制权和⼈才。

### Page 3

阿莫代伊兄妹和另外五位联合创始⼈，通过  Founder LLC 持有⼀股特殊的  F 类股票，这⼀股股
票就拥有公司  50.1% 的总投票权。
⽽掏出  260 亿美元参与私募的英伟达、微软、⾕歌等七⼤科技巨头，拿到的全都是没有投票权
的  N 类股票。
⼆级市场普通股⺠买到的  A 类股，虽然名义上有投票权，但⽆法撼动创始团队的投票权优势。
简单说，钱你们出，算⼒你们供，但模型往哪训练、⽅向盘往哪打，必须由创始⼈说了算。
为什么要把控制权设计得这么牢？
因为三年前  OpenAI 那场闹得满城⻛⾬的 “ 董事会政变 ” 给全硅⾕上了⼀课。
2023 年，奥特曼⼀度被董事会罢免，随后在员⼯、投资⼈和微软等多⽅压⼒下回归。

### Page 4

当资本和商业巨头的意志凌驾于技术团队之上时，⼀家  AI 公司随时可能被撕裂。
Anthropic 显然不想重蹈覆辙，它⼲脆在上市之前，就把创始团队的控制权写进了股权结构⾥。
不过，控制权锁得再牢，也解决不了另⼀个现实问题：⼈，才是  AI 公司最贵的资产。
Anthropic 这次上市，也在员⼯流动性上做了特殊安排。
传统科技公司上市，⾼管和员⼯⼿⾥拿的股票通常有半年的禁售期，半年后才能卖， Anthropic
这次则给员⼯开了⼀个相当特殊的⼝⼦。

### Page 5

上市第⼀天就允许⽼股东和员⼯抛售最多  3.15 亿股，并在第⼀年密集安排了  23 个套现窗⼝。
要知道，现在硅⾕抢⼀个顶尖的⼤模型研究员，开出的薪酬包动辄千万美元起步。
Anthropic 不画⼤饼，上市⾸⽇就直接把纸⾯富贵变成真⾦⽩银，据有关传闻，  5300 名员⼯⾥
⼀⼝⽓就造出了  600 多位亿万富翁和  2800 位千万富翁。
如果说股权设计展现了  Anthropic 的精明，那么它与硅⾕科技巨头之间的利益捆绑，则暴露了它
更⼤的野⼼：提前锁定  AI 时代最稀缺的基础设施。
这也是整份招股书最具争议的地⽅。
很多财经媒体⽤了⼀个词：" 左⼿倒右⼿ " （华尔街称之为 “ 循环融资 ” ）。

### Page 6

从资⾦流向上看，英伟达、⾕歌、 AMD 、微软等七⼤巨头掏出  260 亿美元认购了  Anthropic 的
股票。
转过头， Anthropic 就在招股书⾥签下了⾼达  9009 亿美元的⻓期算⼒与芯⽚采购承诺（其中光
给⾕歌和亚⻢逊的云服务账单就超过  2300 亿美元）。

### Page 7

钱从巨头的投资部⻔流出，兜了⼀圈，⼜变成订单流回了巨头的营收报表⾥。
但换个⻆度看，这也不完全是⼀场资本空转。
毕竟， Anthropic ⼿⾥握着的是实打实的  810 亿美元年化营收和⼀万多家财富  500 强企业客
⼾。
它之所以不惜代价把全硅⾕的芯⽚和云巨头拉进股东名单，本质上是⼤模型军备竞赛进⼊深⽔
区后的 “ 供应链卡位 ” 。
当下⼀代模型的竞争拼的是芯⽚配额、晶圆产能和电⼒能源，⽤股权把供应链上的巨头绑在同
⼀条战船上，就意味着提前锁定了最稀缺的基础设施资源。
到了这个体量，已经没有哪家巨头敢让它输了。
可问题在于，这彻底撕裂了它的⼈设，也难怪会引发近百名  AI 学者痛批它 “ ⾔⾏不⼀ ” 。
⼀边背着近万亿美元的基础设施承诺全速狂奔，⼀边⼜站在道德⾼地上呼吁全⾏业 “ 为了安全慢
下来 ” 。
在开源社区和同⾏眼⾥，这⽆异于 “ ⾃⼰把球场包圆了、把球鞋买光了，然后站出来宣布为了⼤
家的安全，以后必须按我的规则踢球 ” 。

### Page 8

要理解这种撕裂感，还得把时钟拨回  2020 年。
那⼀年，担任  OpenAI 研发副总裁的达⾥奥 · 阿莫代伊，带着担任安全与政策副总裁的妹妹丹妮
拉，以及⼗多位核⼼研究员离开  OpenAI ，并在  2021 年创⽴  Anthropic 。
他们与奥特曼团队的核⼼分歧，正是  AI 商业化与安全之间的平衡。
尤其是微软投资  OpenAI 后，阿莫代伊对公司越来越偏向商业化的⽅向产⽣了担忧。
当时，他们希望建⽴⼀家把  AI 安全放在商业利益之前的公司。
但  5 年过去，剧情却有些微妙。
当年被质疑商业化太激进的  OpenAI ，⾄今还在为上市做准备。
反倒是⾼举安全⼤旗的  Anthropic ，已经抢先敲响了纳斯达克的钟声，两⼈合计⾝家在上市⾸⽇
便突破了  525 亿美元。
七位联合创始⼈公开承诺，未来将把个⼈所持股权价值的  80%  捐献给公益与慈善事业，试图守
住理想主义的最后体⾯。

### Page 9

这背后的变化，或许不只是创始⼈的选择。
当训练前沿模型需要投⼊数百亿美元，算⼒、⼈才和商业规模都成为竞争⻔槛时，只有先把⾃
⼰变成⼀只万亿级别的商业巨兽，才拥有坐上牌桌的资格。
⽽  Anthropic 已经证明，⾃⼰不只有技术理想，也有把技术变成收⼊的能⼒。
招股书⾥， Anthropic 前三季度净亏损  1040 亿美元，乍看很吓⼈。
但其中约  943 亿美元来⾃融资⼯具的账⾯公允价值调整，并⾮实际经营亏损。
按公司披露的调整后⼝径，其营业利润达到  40.8 亿美元，年化营收运⾏率更是达到  810 亿美
元。
在⼀个连搜索引擎都在焦虑如何变现的时代，⼀家成⽴仅⼏年的  AI 公司，凭什么跑出这样的商
业规模？
当竞争对⼿  OpenAI 还在  C 端卷⽤⼾规模时， Anthropic 全⼒押注  B 端⼯程化与  AI Agent （智
能体）。
凭借  Claude Code  等⼯具直接接管企业的软件开发流⽔线，⼀路拿下了超⼀半财富  500 强，
近  2000 家百万美元级超级客⼾，净收⼊留存率更是达到惊⼈的  900%。

Anthropic 的敲钟，或许意味着⼤模型⾏业进⼊了⼀个新阶段。

### Page 10

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
过去，⼤家⽐谁的模型参数更多、跑分更⾼、 Demo 更惊艳。
现在，竞争开始向更深处延伸，谁能拿到更多算⼒，谁能把  AI 真正嵌⼊企业⼯作流，谁⼜能把
技术优势转化为持续收⼊。
Anthropic 已经率先跑通了这条路，接下来的压⼒，⾃然来到了  OpenAI 等竞争对⼿⾝上。
回头看，五年前，阿莫代伊兄妹因为担⼼  AI 过度商业化⽽离开  OpenAI.
五年后你认为  Anthropic 是 “ 屠⻰少年终成恶⻰ ” ，还是⼤模型时代⽣存下去的必然选择？

米国 10 月 ISM 製造業指数は予想を上振れ、仕入価格は高止まり 
～AI・防衛等の需要が底上げ、インフレ懸念に伴う米金利上昇・ドル強含みへ～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治 
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・10 月のISM製造業景気指数は 55.5（前月比＋1.0 ポイント）と市場予想（54.5）を上回り、
10 カ月連続で景気拡大・縮小の分岐点である 50 を上回った。好調なAI需要に加えて、関税引き
上げ前の前倒し発注や地政学リスクを背景とした在庫再構築の動きが、景気を力強く底上げして
いる。 
・AIや防衛など特需分野の急成長が牽引する一方、コスト高や政策警戒感に悩む基礎産業との
「二極化」が鮮明化している。仕入価格指数は 75.2 と高水準で推移し、強いコスト増圧力が持
続している。 
・FRBの追加利上げ観測が高まるなか、製造業の堅調さと価格高止まりはFRBのタカ派スタンス
（金融引き締め継続）を強力に補強する。米長期金利の上昇とドル高の進行が予想される。

1．総合指数は 上昇 し高水準 を維持  
2026 年 10 月のISM製造業景気指数（季節調整値）は 55.5（前月：54.5）と、市場予想中央値
（54.5）を上回り前月比＋1.0 ポイント上昇した。拡大・縮小の分岐点である 50 を 10 カ月連続で
上回り、米製造業の堅調な拡大基調が維持されている。 
足元では、トランプ政権の通商政策に伴う不確実性やコスト増が生産活動の抑制要因となって
いるものの、堅調なAI投資などの国内需要を背景とした在庫補充に加え、関税引き上げや中東情
勢を見越した顧客側の前倒し発注が指数全体を下支えした。

ＩＳＭ製造業景気指数の推移
（注）シャドー部は景気後退期。
(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

2．調査コメントにみる 「業種間の二極化」 と地政学 ・政策 リスク  
パネリストのコメントからは、インフレや政策リスクによるコスト圧力を共通課題としつつ
も、業種ごとに「特需に沸く分野」と「停滞・様子見の分野」への二極化が浮き彫りとなってい
る。

●特需・拡大分野（機械、その他製造業等）： 
AI・データセンター投資、半導体、防衛、自治体インフラ（水関連）などの爆発的な成長に牽
引され、旺盛な需要が継続している。一方で、急激な需要増がサプライチェーンをひっ迫させて
おり、部品のリードタイムの深刻な長期化（4 週間から 50 週間への伸長）や価格高騰、納期クレ
ームなどの課題が浮上している。

●停滞・静観分野（輸送機器、コンピュータ・電子製品等）： 
輸送機器では価格高騰に対する顧客の警戒感が強く、年末の予算消化特需の消失や貨物需要の
低下が目立っている。電子製品でも中東・東欧等の地政学リスクや研究資金への懸念から、新規
装置の導入を見送り既存ツールの最適化に留める「静観モード」が広がっている。

●コスト高・基礎産業（化学製品、食品・飲料・タバコ、金属製品、一次金属等）： 
想定を超えるコスト増（6％以上）、軽油高、トラック運転手不足、対カナダ関税の影響が直撃
している。また、ワシントンの経済政策に対する不透明感から顧客が超短期契約へシフトしてお
り、長期的な経営予測を一層困難にさせている。

ＩＳＭ景気指数の推移
生産
新規受注
（注）シャドー部は景気後退期(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成
3．主要な 構成項目 が拡大圏 を維持  
10 月の総合景気指数を項目別にみると、雇用の鈍化が押し下げ要因となったものの、生産、新
規受注、在庫、サプライヤーの入荷遅延の強まりが全体を押し上げた。一方、価格指数は前月よ
り若干低下したものの依然として 75 超の極めて高い水準にあり、製造業におけるコスト増圧力の
根強さを示している。 
●総合指数への寄与度（前月比＋1.0 ポイント）  
雇用（▲0.06 ポイント）が押し下げに寄与した一方、在庫（＋0.56 ポイント）、生産（＋0.46
ポイント）、新規受注（＋0.04 ポイント）、入荷遅延（＋0.02 ポイント）のプラス寄与が押し上げ
に貢献した（四捨五入の影響を含む）。新規受注、生産、雇用、在庫、入荷遅延はいずれも 50 を
超える拡大圏を維持している。

●主要項目の詳細動向 
・新規受注（55.5/前月：55.3）：前月から小幅上昇し、高い水準を維持して需要の堅調さを示して
いる。ただし、AI 関連需要に加えて、米国・イラン交渉の停滞に伴う価格上昇や供給懸念に先んじ
た前倒し発注が下支え要因となっている面もある。全 18 業種中 11 業種で拡大、縮小は 4 業種。

・生産（59.0/前月：56.7）：高水準に上昇し、生産活動の強まりを示している。拡大は 11 業種、縮
小は 3 業種。新規受注および受注残が拡大水準を維持していることから、生産拡大の持続性は高い
とみられる。

・雇用（52.4/前月：52.7）：前月から低下したものの、拡大圏を維持した。労働市場の緩やかな改
善を示唆する一方、拡大 8 業種に対し縮小が 5 業種となった。マクロ環境としては、需要動向の不
透明感を背景に、企業がレイオフや採用凍結を通じた人員・人件費調整を優先する姿勢を継続して
いると考えられる。

ＩＳＭ景気指数の推移
在庫
入荷遅延
（注）シャドー部は景気後退期(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成
・在庫（51.4/前月：48.6）：拡大圏へ上昇した。地政学的な不確実性の高まりから慎重な在庫管
理姿勢が続くものの、手元在庫の減少や顧客側の在庫不足感を背景に一定の積み増しが進んでい
る。なお、顧客在庫指数は低下傾向が続いており（適正水準を下回る状態）、将来的な需要回復期
における在庫の再積み増しが今後の生産を刺激する潜在的な下支え要因となる。

・入荷遅延（59.1/前月：59.0）：小幅に上昇し、依然として 50 を大きく上回っている。港湾での
停滞やトラック輸送の制約、悪天候に加え、イランを含む中東情勢の混乱が、サプライヤーの納
入期間を長期化させる主因となっている。

・貿易関連：サブ項目をみると、新規輸出受注が 51.2（前月：50.9）、輸入指数は 52.9（前月：
51.0）とそれぞれ拡大圏で上昇した。輸入は、サプライチェーン寸断への懸念から物資を前倒し
で確保する動きにも支えられ高水準を維持した。

4．業種 別の広がり （12 業種 が拡大 ） 
10 月に総合指数が拡大した業種は、全 18 業種のうち、プラスチック・ゴム製品、電気機器・電
化製品・電気部品、一般機械、コンピュータ・電子製品、繊維、一次金属、輸送機器、加工金
属、家具・同関連製品、化学製品、その他製造業、食品・飲料・タバコ製品の 12 業種（前月：12
業種）となった。 
主要 6 業種のうち拡大した業種は、石油・石炭製品を除く 5 業種（コンピュータ・電子製品、
一般機械、輸送機器、化学製品、食品・飲料・タバコ製品）である。一方、縮小した業種は印

ＩＳＭ景気指数の推移
仕入価格
入荷遅延
（注）シャドー部は景気後退期(出所)ＩＳＭより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成
刷・関連支援活動、紙製品、非金属鉱物製品の 3 業種（前月：2 業種）に増加した。なお、石油・
石炭製品、木材製品、アパレル・皮革製品は前月から横ばいとなっている。

5．高止まりする 仕入価格 と供給不足  
インフレ動向を示す仕入価格指数は、75.2（前月：77.9）と小幅低下したものの、依然として
極めて高い水準にとどまっている。10 月に物価上昇を報告した回答者の割合は 53.1％となり、9
月の 58.6％から低下したものの、製造業における強いコスト増圧力が持続していることを裏付け
ている。鉄鋼やアルミニウム価格の上昇、関税、中東情勢の緊迫化を受けた石油製品の高騰が仕
入価格指数を押し上げている。価格上昇を報告した業種は 16 業種（前月：16 業種）に及び、価格
下落を報告した業種はゼロであった。  
商品別では、アルミニウム、銅、鉄鋼・同製品、電子・メモリ部品、半導体、プリント基板、
熱間圧延鋼、樹脂、ステンレス鋼、貨物（運賃）、燃料、石油製品、ディーゼル燃料、段ボール製
品など広範囲な品目で価格上昇が確認された。また、供給不足品目として電子部品、電気部品、
メモリ、プリント基板、集積回路といったAI関連のほか、鉄鋼、銅、アルミニウム製品が挙げら
れ、トランプ政権の対外・通商政策に伴う供給下押し圧力が継続している。

6．先行き展望：短期的混乱と中期的な拡大基調  
今後の製造業の業況について、短期的には米国とイランの停戦合意の履行や和平交渉を巡る不
確実性が足かせとなるものの、一段のコスト上昇を警戒した需要の前倒しが当面の下支え要因に
なると見込まれる。 
中期的には、①半導体、医薬品、重要鉱物等への分野別関税によるインフレ波及が限定的とな
る公算が高いこと、②AI投資に加えて減税や給与所得・資産価格の上昇を背景に国内需要が堅調

を維持すること、③低水準にある顧客在庫が生産を誘発すること等に支えられ、製造業は 2027 年
前半にかけても拡大の勢いを保つ可能性が高いと考えられる。

7．FRB の政策 スタンスと 金融 市場 への影響  
9 月の利上げ実施に続き、FRBによる追加利上げ観測が市場で高まるなか、ISM製造業の強さ
（55.5）と仕入価格の高止まり（75.2）は、根強いインフレ圧力を実証した。このようなデータ
は、FRBがタカ派スタンス（引き締め継続）を維持し、追加利上げへ踏み切る強い正当性を与え
る。潜在成長率を超える景気拡大とインフレ圧力の再燃を背景に、FRBは 12 月FOMCで 25bpの追加
利上げを実施する見込みである。2027 年も堅調な経済と鈍いインフレ低下を背景に、政策金利は
高い水準で維持される可能性が高い。 
FRBのターミナルレート（最終到達金利）切り上げ観測や高金利環境の長期化が予想されるな
か、米 10 年債利回りには上昇圧力がかかりやすく、内外金利差の拡大を通じて為替市場ではドル
強含みの展開が予想される。

消費者物価
米国コア CPI が 3 カ月連続で予想を上振れ(2026 年 10 月) 
～12 月 FOMC での 25bp の利上げが濃厚、一部で 50bp の利上げ警戒～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治 
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨 
・2026 年 10 月の米コアCPIは前月比＋0.3％と市場予想（＋0.2％）を 3 カ月連続で上回り、
インフレの粘着性が改めて浮き彫りとなった。

・食品価格の上昇により総合CPIは市場予想を上回った一方、サービスインフレ（とりわけ「スー
パーコア」）が高止まりを続けており、基調的なインフレ低下プロセスは完全に停滞している。

・インフレのモメンタムが加速（3 カ月前対比年率＋3.7％）したことを受け、FRBが 12 月
FOMCで追加利上げ（25bp）を実施する確度は大幅に高まった。市場では 50bpの大幅利上げ
が意識され金利上昇、ドル高、株安圧力が強まるものの、12 月の利上げの織り込みが 25bpに落
ち着くにつれ調整に向かう（市場の動揺は和らぐ）見通しだ。

1.  コア CPI は 3 カ月連続で 予想を 上振れ  
2026 年 10 月の米消費者物価指数（総合CPI）は、前月比＋0.4％（前月：＋0.6％）となり、市
場予想中央値（＋0.3％）および筆者予想（＋0.3％）を上回った。一方、エネルギー・食品を除

### Page 2

2/9 
く消費者物価指数（コアCPI）は、前月比＋0.3％（前月：＋0.3％）と高水準を維持した。前月と
同率の伸びとなり、市場予想（＋0.2％）を 3 カ月連続で上回った。小数点第 3 位まで確認する
と、前月比＋0.296％（前月：＋0.335％）とわずかに減速したものの、依然として 2％目標に整合
的な水準を大幅に上回っており、足元でのインフレ圧力の再燃が確認された。なお、前年同月比
については、前年 10 月の政府機関閉鎖に伴うデータ未収集の影響で今回公表が見送られたが、前
年 10 月の賃貸料・帰属家賃が低めに算出されたことから、前月（9 月）より伸び率が拡大してい
る可能性が高い。 
内訳をみると、食品価格は、穀物・ベーカリー製品が横ばいとなった一方、肉類・乳製品が上
昇に転じたほか、野菜・果物、外食の伸び率が高まり、全体として前月比＋0.5％（前月：
0.0％）とプラス幅を拡大した。 
エネルギー価格は電気料金が前月比＋0.9％と伸び率を高めたものの、ガソリン（前月比＋
1.0％、前月：＋9.1％）の伸びが大幅に鈍化したことで、全体としては前月比＋0.7％（前月：＋
5.2％）とプラス幅を縮小し、総合CPIの上昇抑制に寄与した。

2.  コア CP Iでは、 財、サービス ともに 伸び 鈍化 も、ス ーパーコアの 粘着性 が継続  
コアCPIの内訳では、財コアが前月比＋0.2％（前月：＋0.3％）、サービスコアが同＋0.3％（前
月：＋0.4％）と、ともに前月から伸び率が鈍化した。 
財コアでは、家庭用耐久品・消耗品、医療用品が上昇に転じたほか、教材、その他財が伸び率
を拡大した。また、新車は前月と同率の伸びにとどまり、アルコール飲料は横ばいとなった。一
方、中古車がマイナスに転じたほか、衣料品、自動車部品、余暇商品、情報機器の伸び率が低下
した。サプライチェーンの正常化等による財価格の押し下げ効果は一巡しつつあり、今後は輸入
物価上昇や在庫再構築に伴う価格転嫁の動きが一部で下支え要因となろう。 
サービスコアでは、専門医療、病院・関連サービス、自動車保険、余暇サービスで伸び率が高
まった。また、帰属家賃および賃貸料は前月比＋0.3％と前月と同率の伸びを保った。一方、レン
タカーが下落に転じ、医療保険、電話サービスは下落幅を拡大した。インターネットサービスも
下落を維持した。さらに、ホテル、上下水道・ゴミ収集サービス、自動車メンテナンス・修理、
航空運賃、授業料・その他の学校納付金・託児費用、その他個人向けサービスは伸び率を縮小さ
せた。なお、カーリースは伸び率が低下して横ばいとなった。 
特にFRBが基調的なインフレ動向として重視する「住宅を除くサービス（スーパーコア）」は、
人件費比率が高い対人サービス業での堅調な賃金の伸びや粘着的な単位労働コスト（ULC）上昇を
背景に、前月比＋0.4％の高水準を維持している。民間の先行指標で示される家賃下落のCPIへの
波及の程度の違いやタイムラグも重なり、サービス物価全般の下げ渋りを強力にサポートしてい
る。これはFRBが最も警戒するインフレの二次的効果（賃金と物価のスパイラル）が継続している
ことを示唆している。

3.  インフレ の上昇モメンタム 加速 、12 月FOMC での 追加 利上 げを後押し  
コアCPIの上昇モメンタムをみると、10 月は 3 カ月前対比年率で＋3.7％（9 月：＋3.4％）と加
速し、6 カ月前対比年率でも＋2.7％（9 月：＋2.9％）と高水準で推移している。特に短期的トレ
ンドを示す 3 カ月前対比年率がFRBのインフレ目標（2.0％）を大きく上回って加速している事実
は、ディスインフレ（物価減速）プロセスが完全に足踏み状態にあることを証明している。この
データは、12 月のFOMCにおいてFRBが追加利上げを検討・実施する極めて強力な根拠となる。

4.  FRB は 12 月FOMC で 25 bp の追加 利上げ 実施へ  
今回のコアCPIの上振れを受け、FF金利先物市場が織り込む 12 月FOMCでの 25bp追加利上げの確
度は、発表前の約 84％から 100％へと上昇した。次回のFOMC（2026 年 12 月 15～16 日）までに確

認できる主要指標としては 11 月雇用統計や 11 月CPIが残されているが、労働市場で政策由来の構
造的な労働供給制約と底堅い労働需要が拮抗する「綱渡りの均衡状態」が続くとみられるなか、
FRBが景気の安定維持や、「インフレの上振れリスクを未然に防ぎ、期待インフレ率をアンカーす
る」観点から、12 月に 25bpの追加利上げ（政策金利誘導目標を 4.00～4.25％へ引き上げ）に踏み
切る可能性が極めて高いと判断される。
また、一部のFRB高官によるタカ派発言に市場が過剰反応し、50bpの大幅利上げの可能性が意識

され始めている。これに伴い、目先の米金利には上昇圧力がかかりやすく、ドル高の進行や株式
市場の上値を抑える要因として作用すると見込まれる。ただし、最終的に市場の織り込みが 25bp
の利上げに落ち着く過程で、短期金利の水準は適正化され、ドルも次第に上値の重い展開へ移行
すると想定される。

Document 726: 統計通報-健身風氣益為盛行，114年健身中心家數及銷售額俱創新高 (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/統計通報-健身風氣益為盛行，114年健身中心家數及銷售額俱創新高 (2026-10-22).pdf

Date: 2026-10-22

### Page 1

財政統計通報

（第 17 號） 
財政部統計處  撰稿人：林芝螢助理研究員 
115 年 10 月 22 日  聯絡人：侯永盛 科長 
聯絡電話：(02)23228346  
健身風氣益為盛行，114 年健身中心家數及銷售額俱創新高 
1.隨著國人健康意識提升及運動風氣逐漸普及，帶動各式運動場館發展。依據我國營利事業家
數及銷售額統計，近 5 年運動場館 1家數由 2,106 家增至 3,069 家，銷售額由 298.9 億元增
至 426.0 億元，分別成長 45.7%及 42.5%，均以健身中心為主要貢獻來源。114 年健身中心
計有 1,127 家，創下歷年新高(較 109 年增 54.8%)，今(115)年 7 月底再增至 1,166 家，占整
體運動場館近 4 成，居各類運動場館之冠，主因著重一般性體能訓練，且場地不受氣候影
響，較能切合普羅大眾之健身需求，加上健身產業服務型態日益多元，除傳統健身器材訓練
外，亦涵蓋團體課程、私人教練等服務，且積極拓展消費族群等因素所致。銷售額方面，近
年除 110 年受新冠疫情影響下滑至低點外，大致呈現逐年成長，114 年為 212.5 億元，首度
突破 200 億元，5 年來增加 39.7%，今年前 7 月續增 12.6%。

2.按區域分布觀察，健身中心主要集中於人口及商業活動較為密集的 6 都，114 年計 840 家，
占全國 74.5%，其中臺北 181 家最多，占 16.1%，其次為臺中 173 家(占 15.4%)，銷售額集
中情形更為明顯，6 都占全國 8 成 2，以臺北 40.3 億元居冠，新北及臺中居 2、3 位。6 都與
109 年比較，桃園之家數成長逾 1.6 倍，增幅最高，臺中亦增 71.3%；銷售額增幅則介於
25%~53%。若折算平均每家健身中心年銷售額，114 年以新北 2,501 萬元最高，桃園 1,438
萬元最低，且較 109 年減少逾 4 成，可能與近年新進業者增加，市場競爭更為激烈有關。

Document 752: 英伟达拟5亿美元收购扩散模型初创公司Inception，押注AI提速技术

Source PDF: all report pdf/英伟达拟5亿美元收购扩散模型初创公司Inception，押注AI提速技术.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

扩散模型⽐主流⾃回归模型⽣成速度更快，尤其擅⻓⻓⽂本输出。此次收购将强化
Nemotron 开源模型，直接对抗 OpenAI 与 Anthropic ，并延伸⾄机器⼈ “ 物理模型 ” 领域。
摘要：
英伟达正就收购 AI 初创公司 Inception 展开深度洽谈，交易估值⾄少 5 亿美元，另附基于业绩⽬标
的额外奖励安排。此举旨在为英伟达旗下开源模型项⽬ Nemotron 引⼊扩散模型技术与核⼼⼈
才。
据科技媒体 The Information 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇报道，三名知情⼈⼠透露，上述谈判已进⼊⾼级阶段。
Inception 的扩散⼤语⾔模型技术及其背后的研究团队，预计将直接服务于英伟达 Nemotron 开
源模型的研发。谈判在英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋离任之前已经启动，⽬前尚⽆迹象显⽰英伟达将因领
导层变动⽽退出。
值得关注的是，微软此前也曾就类似交易与 Inception 接触，但最终未能推进。
扩散模型：⽐⾃回归更快的语⾔ AI 路径
据报道，知情⼈⼠指出，扩散模型相较于⾃回归模型的最⼤优势在于速度，尤其在⽣成较⻓回

复时，速度优势更为显著。
扩散技术此前主要应⽤于图像⽣成领域，近年来才被 Inception 等初创公司以及⾕歌 DeepMind 等
⼤型开发商引⼊语⾔⽣成 AI 。今年 5 ⽉，英伟达已发布⼀个将扩散与⾃回归及其他传统语⾔ AI 形
式相结合的 Nemotron 版本。
Inception 成⽴约两年，总部位于旧⾦⼭湾区，现有员⼯逾 30 ⼈，此前已从 Menlo Ventures 、
M12 、 Mayfield 以及英伟达等投资⽅融资 5000 万美元。
该公司于 2025 年初凭借⼀款扩散⼤语⾔模型引发业界关注。与 ChatGPT 、 Claude 等产品所采⽤
的 " ⾃回归 " 模型从左⾄右逐词⽣成答案的⽅式不同，扩散语⾔模型先⽣成⼀个看似杂乱的完整答
案，再经过反复精炼得出最终输出。
据报道，⼀名知情⼈⼠透露，Inception 联合创始⼈ Stefano Ermon 、 Aditya Grover 和
Volodymyr Kuleshov 预计将全职加⼊英伟达。三⼈分别是斯坦福⼤学、加州⼤学洛杉矶分校和

康奈尔⼤学的知名机器学习教授。
知情⼈⼠还表⽰，上述研究⼈员在英伟达还计划将其⼯作延伸⾄所谓的 “ 物理模型 ” 领域，以帮助
机器⼈感知和处理周围环境。
Nemotron ：对抗 Anthropic 与 OpenAI 的开源战略
此次洽谈是英伟达持续扩充 Nemotron 团队系列动作的最新⼀环。据报道，英伟达已累计投⼊数
百亿美元⽤于 Nemotron 相关模型，其中包括斥资数⼗亿美元收购或准收购技术与 AI 研究⼈员，
Nemotron 相关团队⽬前已有数百名员⼯。
Nemotron 模型的战略定位，是为企业和政府客⼾提供有别于 Anthropic 、 OpenAI 等闭源 AI 的
替代⽅案，同时推动英伟达在机器⼈ AI 领域的布局。 Booz Allen 、 Palantir 以及多个不愿使⽤中
国开源模型的美国政府机构，均已是 Nemotron 的⽤⼾。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
